# Bhagavad Gītā

`src:bhagavad-gita` · `sourced` · confidence high

**Alternate titles:** Gītā, Śrīmad Bhagavad Gītā, Gītopaniṣad, Harigītā (as the Nārāyaṇīya refers to Kṛṣṇa's teaching)
**Original title:** भगवद्गीता
**Language:** Sanskrit
**Family:** vedic
**Lineages:** [Epic teaching (the Mahābhārata's Sāṃkhya-Yoga and Mokṣadharma milieu)](../lineages/epic-teaching.md), [Early Bhāgavata devotion (the Nārāyaṇīya / Sātvata–Ekāntika dharma)](../lineages/bhagavata-early.md), [Vedānta (Uttara Mīmāṃsā)](../lineages/vedanta.md)
**Genre:** gītā (dialogue); smṛti-prasthāna of Vedānta
**Part of:** [Bhīṣmaparvan](bhismaparvan.md)
**Location in parent:** Bhīṣmaparvan adhyāyas 23–40 (Critical Edition) = 25–42 (vulgate)
**Authors:** 
  - [Kṛṣṇa (Vāsudeva)](../teachers/krsna.md) — role: revealer; attribution: traditional
  - [Vyāsa (Kṛṣṇa Dvaipāyana)](../teachers/vyasa.md) — role: author; attribution: traditional
**Attribution:** tradition: Spoken by Kṛṣṇa (the Lord) to Arjuna, heard and relayed to Dhṛtarāṣṭra by Sañjaya through the divine sight granted by Vyāsa (18.75), and set in the Mahābhārata by Vyāsa. The chapter colophons call it an Upaniṣad, a knowledge of Brahman (brahmavidyā) and a treatise on yoga (yogaśāstra).; scholarly: An integral part of the Bhīṣmaparvan whose unity and date are debated; many scholars see a single composition drawing on Upaniṣadic, Sāṃkhya-Yoga and Bhāgavata materials, others posit layers.; confidence: moderate
**Dates:** Tradition's account: spoken on the first day of the Kurukṣetra war at the end of the Dvāpara yuga (before the Kali yuga, traditionally reckoned from 3102 BCE); Scholarly account: c. 2nd c. BCE – 2nd c. CE (estimates range from the 5th c. BCE to the 3rd c. CE); (confidence moderate)
**Structure:** 18 chapters, 700 verses in the vulgate and the Critical Edition (a verse at the head of ch. 13 in some printed editions makes 701). The Kashmir recension (commented on by Abhinavagupta and Rājānaka Rāmakaṇṭha) has additional verses and many variant readings. A verse found in some manuscripts (the 'Gītāmāna') speaks of 745 verses (620 by Kṛṣṇa, 57 by Arjuna, 67 by Sañjaya, 1 by Dhṛtarāṣṭra); it is not in the Critical Edition.
**Availability:** digitized-original

The dialogue in which Kṛṣṇa, as charioteer, instructs Arjuna on the battlefield of Kurukṣetra after Arjuna refuses to fight: the imperishable self, action without attachment to its fruits (karma-yoga), knowledge, meditation and devotion to the Lord as paths, the Lord's two natures, his descents and his universal form, the field and its knower, the three guṇas, the divine and demonic endowments, the threefold faith, and renunciation, ending in the call to take refuge in him alone (18.66).
**Editions / translations:** 
  - kind: original; name: Bhīṣmaparvan 23–40 in the Mahābhārata Critical Edition, ed. S. K. Belvalkar, BORI (1945–47); licence: print
  - kind: original; name: gita/gita JSON corpus (Sanskrit text, transliteration, several commentaries); licence: Unlicense; url: https://github.com/gita/gita
  - kind: original; name: Gita Press, Gorakhpur editions (vulgate; with Śaṅkara's and other commentaries); licence: print
  - kind: original; name: F. O. Schrader, The Kashmir Recension of the Bhagavadgītā (Stuttgart, 1930); licence: print
**Commentaries on this text:** [Anāsakti Yoga of M. K. Gandhi](anasakti-yoga.md), [Bhagavad-gītā As It Is](bhagavad-gita-as-it-is.md), [Bhagavadgītā Vīraśaiva-bhāṣya (T. G. Siddappārādhya)](bhagavadgita-virasaiva-bhasya.md), [Essays on the Gita of Sri Aurobindo](essays-on-the-gita.md), [Gītābhāṣya of Bhāskara](gita-bhasya-bhaskara.md), [Gītābhāṣya (Madhva)](gita-bhasya-madhva.md), [Gītābhāṣya of Rāmānuja](gita-bhasya-ramanuja.md), [Gītābhāṣya of Śaṅkara](gita-bhasya-sankara.md), [Gītābhūṣaṇa of Baladeva Vidyābhūṣaṇa](gita-bhusana.md), [Gītā Rahasya (Karmayogaśāstra) of Bāl Gaṅgādhar Tilak](gita-rahasya.md), [Gītātātparyanirṇaya](gita-tatparya-nirnaya.md), [Gītārthasaṅgraha](gitarthasangraha.md), [Gītārthasaṅgraha of Abhinavagupta](gitarthasangraha-abhinavagupta.md), [Gītārthasaṅgraha of Yāmuna](gitarthasangraha-yamuna.md), [Gītāvivṛti](gitavivrti.md), [Gūḍhārthadīpikā](gudharthadipika.md), [Jñāneśvarī (Bhāvārthadīpikā)](jnanesvari.md), [Kathā-Gītā (of Bhaṭṭadeva)](katha-gita-bhattadeva.md), [Sārārthavarṣiṇī](sarartha-varsini.md), [Sārārthavarṣiṇī of Viśvanātha Cakravartin](sararthavarsini.md), [Sarvatobhadra of Rājānaka Rāmakaṇṭha](sarvatobhadra-ramakantha.md), [Subodhinī of Śrīdhara Svāmin](subodhini-sridhara.md), [Tattvaprakāśikā of Keśava Kāśmīrī Bhaṭṭa](tattvaprakasika-kesava-kasmiri.md), [Tattvaprakāśikā (Gītā commentary of Keśava Kāśmīrin)](tattvaprakasika-kesava-kasmirin.md), [Tattvārthadīpanibandha of Vallabha](tattvartha-dipa-nibandha.md)

## Teachings (618: text-verified 386, sourced 232)

### 1.1 <a id="tea-bhagavad-gita-1-1"></a>
`text-verified` · confidence high

Dhṛtarāṣṭra asks Sañjaya: in the field of dharma (dharmakṣetra), Kurukṣetra, assembled and eager to fight, what did my people (māmakāḥ) and the sons of Pāṇḍu (pāṇḍavāḥ) do?

> dharmakṣetre kurukṣetre samavetā yuyutsavaḥ|
māmakāḥ pāṇḍavāścaiva kimakurvata sañjaya

_level: unmarked · standpoint: ethical-social · path: general · stage: unmarked · types: narrative, teacher-transmission_

terms: [dharma](../terms/dharma.md) · teachers: [Dhṛtarāṣṭra](../teachers/dhrtarastra.md), [Sañjaya](../teachers/sanjaya.md), [Pāṇḍu](../teachers/pandu.md)

### 1.2 <a id="tea-bhagavad-gita-1-2"></a>
`text-verified` · confidence high

Sañjaya: having seen the army of the Pāṇḍavas drawn up in battle array, King Duryodhana then approached the teacher (ācārya) and spoke these words.

> dṛṣṭvā tu pāṇḍavānīkaṃ vyūḍhaṃ duryodhanastadā|
ācāryamupasaṅgamya rājā vacanamabravīt

_level: unmarked · standpoint: ethical-social · path: general · stage: unmarked · types: narrative_

teachers: [Sañjaya](../teachers/sanjaya.md), [Duryodhana](../teachers/duryodhana.md), [Droṇa](../teachers/drona.md)

### 1.3 <a id="tea-bhagavad-gita-1-3"></a>
`text-verified` · confidence high

Duryodhana to the teacher: behold, teacher, this great army of the sons of Pāṇḍu, arrayed by the son of Drupada, your intelligent disciple (śiṣya).

> paśyaitāṃ pāṇḍuputrāṇāmācārya mahatīṃ camūm|
vyūḍhāṃ drupadaputreṇa tava śiṣyeṇa dhīmatā

_level: unmarked · standpoint: ethical-social · path: general · stage: unmarked · types: narrative_

terms: [śiṣya](../terms/sisya.md) · teachers: [Duryodhana](../teachers/duryodhana.md), [Droṇa](../teachers/drona.md), [Dhṛṣṭadyumna](../teachers/dhrstadyumna.md), [Drupada](../teachers/drupada.md), [Pāṇḍu](../teachers/pandu.md)

### 1.4 <a id="tea-bhagavad-gita-1-4"></a>
`text-verified` · confidence high

Duryodhana: here are heroes, great archers, equal in battle to Bhīma and Arjuna — Yuyudhāna, Virāṭa, and Drupada the great chariot-warrior (mahāratha);

> atra śūrā maheṣvāsā bhīmārjunasamā yudhi|
yuyudhāno virāṭaśca drupadaśca mahārathaḥ

_level: unmarked · standpoint: ethical-social · path: general · stage: unmarked · types: narrative_

`text-verified` · confidence high

Duryodhana continues the list of the Pāṇḍava warriors: Dhṛṣṭaketu, Cekitāna and the valiant king of Kāśī (kāśirāja); Purujit, Kuntibhoja and Śaibya, a bull among men.

> dhṛṣṭaketuścekitānaḥ kāśirājaśca vīryavān|
purujitkuntibhojaśca śaibyaśca narapuṅgavaḥ

`text-verified` · confidence high

Duryodhana: but know, best of the twice-born (dvijottama), those who are most distinguished among us, the leaders of my army; I name them to you for your information.

> asmākaṃ tu viśiṣṭā ye tānnibodha dvijottama|
nāyakā mama sainyasya saṃjñārthaṃ tānbravīmi te

### 1.8 <a id="tea-bhagavad-gita-1-8"></a>
`text-verified` · confidence high

Duryodhana names the leaders of his own army: yourself (Droṇa), Bhīṣma, Karṇa and Kṛpa, victorious in battle; Aśvatthāman, Vikarṇa, and likewise the son of Somadatta (saumadatti).

> bhavānbhīṣmaśca karṇaśca kṛpaśca samitiñjayaḥ|
aśvatthāmā vikarṇaśca saumadattistathaiva ca

Duryodhana: and many other heroes who have given up their lives for my sake (madarthe tyakta-jīvitāḥ), bearing various weapons, all skilled in war.

### 1.10 <a id="tea-bhagavad-gita-1-10"></a>
`text-verified` · confidence moderate

Duryodhana compares the two forces: 'aparyāpta' is that force of ours protected by Bhīṣma, while 'paryāpta' is this force of theirs protected by Bhīma.

> aparyāptaṃ tadasmākaṃ balaṃ bhīṣmābhirakṣitam|
paryāptaṃ tvidameteṣāṃ balaṃ bhīmābhirakṣitam

> tasya saṃjanayanharṣaṃ kuruvṛddhaḥ pitāmahaḥ|

Arjuna: '— so that I may observe these who stand here eager to fight, and see with whom I must fight in this undertaking of battle.'

`text-verified` · confidence high

Arjuna: 'I want to see those who are about to fight, who have gathered here wishing to do in battle what pleases the evil-minded (durbuddhi) son of Dhṛtarāṣṭra.'

> yotsyamānānavekṣe'haṃ ya ete'tra samāgatāḥ|
dhārtarāṣṭrasya durbuddheryuddhe priyacikīrṣavaḥ

### 2.1 <a id="tea-bhagavad-gita-2-1"></a>
`text-verified` · confidence high

Sañjaya: to him, thus overcome by pity (kṛpā), his eyes filled with tears and troubled, despairing (viṣīdantam), Madhusūdana spoke these words:

> taṃ tathā kṛpayā'viṣṭamaśrupūrṇākulekṣaṇam|
viṣīdantamidaṃ vākyamuvāca madhusūdanaḥ

_level: unmarked · standpoint: experiential · path: general · stage: unmarked · types: narrative_

terms: [kṛpā](../terms/krpa.md), [viṣāda](../terms/visada.md) · obstacles: [Despondency (viṣāda)](../obstacles/visada.md) · teachers: [Sañjaya](../teachers/sanjaya.md), [Kṛṣṇa (Vāsudeva)](../teachers/krsna.md), [Arjuna (Pārtha, Dhanaṃjaya)](../teachers/arjuna.md)

### 2.2 <a id="tea-bhagavad-gita-2-2"></a>
`text-verified` · confidence high

Kṛṣṇa: from where has this faintness (kaśmala) come upon you at this critical moment (viṣame), Arjuna? It is not practised by the noble (anārya-juṣṭa), does not lead to heaven (asvargya), and brings disgrace (akīrti-kara).

> śrī bhagavānuvāca
kutastvā kaśmalamidaṃ viṣame samupasthitam|
anāryajuṣṭamasvargyamakīrtikaramarjuna

_level: conventional · standpoint: ethical-social · path: general · stage: unmarked · types: ethics_

terms: [kaśmala](../terms/kasmala.md), [svarga](../terms/svarga.md) · obstacles: [Faintness, dejection (kaśmala)](../obstacles/kasmala.md) · teachers: [Kṛṣṇa (Vāsudeva)](../teachers/krsna.md), [Arjuna (Pārtha, Dhanaṃjaya)](../teachers/arjuna.md) · disputes: [Should Arjuna fight the battle in which his elders, teachers and kinsmen stand on the other side?](../debates/bhagavad-gita-arjuna-should-fight.md)

### 2.3 <a id="tea-bhagavad-gita-2-3"></a>
`text-verified` · confidence high

Kṛṣṇa: do not yield to unmanliness (klaibya), Pārtha; it does not befit you. Cast off this petty weakness of heart (hṛdaya-daurbalya) and stand up, scorcher of foes!

> klaibyaṃ mā sma gamaḥ pārtha naitattvayyupapadyate|
kṣudraṃ hṛdayadaurbalyaṃ tyaktvottiṣṭha parantapa

_level: conventional · standpoint: ethical-social · path: action · stage: unmarked · types: ethics_

### 2.4 <a id="tea-bhagavad-gita-2-4"></a>
`text-verified` · confidence high

Arjuna: how shall I, Madhusūdana, fight back with arrows in battle against Bhīṣma and Droṇa, who are worthy of worship (pūjārha), slayer of enemies?

> kathaṃ bhīṣmamahaṃ saṃkhye droṇaṃ ca madhusūdana|
iṣubhiḥ pratiyotsyāmi pūjārhāvarisūdana

_level: conventional · standpoint: ethical-social · path: general · stage: unmarked · types: ethics_

### 2.5 <a id="tea-bhagavad-gita-2-5"></a>
`text-verified` · confidence moderate

Arjuna: better in this world to eat even alms-food (bhaikṣya) than to kill these venerable (mahānubhāva) gurus; for having killed the gurus — 'arthakāmān' — I would enjoy, here in this very world (ihaiva), pleasures smeared with blood.

> gurūnahatvā hi mahānubhāvān
śreyo bhoktuṃ bhaikṣyamapīha loke|
hatvārthakāmāṃstu gurūnihaiva
bhuñjīya bhogān rudhirapradigdhān

_level: conventional · standpoint: ethical-social · path: general · stage: unmarked · types: ethics_

### 2.6 <a id="tea-bhagavad-gita-2-6"></a>
`text-verified` · confidence high

Arjuna: nor do we know which is weightier (garīyas) for us — whether we should conquer or they should conquer us. The very sons of Dhṛtarāṣṭra, having killed whom we would not wish to live, stand facing us.

> na caitadvidmaḥ kataranno garīyo
yadvā jayema yadi vā no jayeyuḥ|
yāneva hatvā na jijīviṣāma
ste'vasthitāḥ pramukhe dhārtarāṣṭrāḥ

_level: conventional · standpoint: ethical-social · path: general · stage: unmarked · types: ethics_

### 2.7 <a id="tea-bhagavad-gita-2-7"></a>
`text-verified` · confidence high

Arjuna: my very nature struck by the fault of pitiable weakness (kārpaṇya-doṣa), my mind bewildered about dharma (dharma-saṃmūḍha-cetāḥ), I ask you: tell me decisively what is better (śreyas). I am your disciple (śiṣya); instruct me, who have taken refuge in you (tvāṃ prapannam).

> kārpaṇyadoṣopahatasvabhāvaḥ
pṛcchāmi tvāṃ dharmasaṃmūḍhacetāḥ|
yacchreyaḥ syānniśicataṃ brūhi tanme
śiṣyaste'haṃ śādhi māṃ tvāṃ prapannam

_level: conventional · standpoint: seeker · path: general · stage: beginner · types: teacher-transmission, ethics_

`text-verified` · confidence high

Arjuna: for I do not see what would drive away this grief (śoka) of mine that dries up the senses (indriya), even if I were to obtain on earth an unrivalled, prosperous kingdom, or even lordship over the gods.

> na hi prapaśyāmi mamāpanudyā
dyacchokamucchoṣaṇamindriyāṇām|
avāpya bhūmāvasapatnamṛddham
rājyaṃ surāṇāmapi cādhipatyam

### 2.11 <a id="tea-bhagavad-gita-2-11"></a>
`text-verified` · confidence moderate

Kṛṣṇa: you have grieved for those who should not be grieved for, and yet you speak words of wisdom (prajñāvāda). The wise (paṇḍita) grieve neither for those whose life has gone nor for those whose life has not gone.

> śrī bhagavānuvāca
aśocyānanvaśocastvaṃ prajñāvādāṃśca bhāṣase|
gatāsūnagatāsūṃśca nānuśocanti paṇḍitāḥ

### 2.11-30 <a id="tea-bhagavad-gita-2-11-30"></a>
`sourced` · confidence high

The teaching on the self: the wise grieve neither for the living nor for the dead; the embodied self was never non-existent and will never cease; it passes through the bodily stages and into another body; sense-contacts are transient and to be endured; the unreal has no being and the real never ceases; the self is unborn, eternal, not slain when the body is slain, not cut, burned, wetted or dried, unmanifest and unthinkable; therefore one should not grieve for any being.

_level: ultimate · standpoint: substance · path: knowledge · stage: all · types: ultimate, death-dying_

terms: [ātman](../terms/atman.md), [dehin](../terms/dehin.md) · concepts: [The self](../concepts/the-self.md)

_Superseded by [2.11](bhagavad-gita.md#tea-bhagavad-gita-2-11)_

Kṛṣṇa: as in this body the embodied one (dehin) passes through childhood, youth and old age, so too there is the attaining of another body; the steadfast one (dhīra) is not bewildered by this.

### 2.14 <a id="tea-bhagavad-gita-2-14"></a>
`text-verified` · confidence moderate

Kṛṣṇa: the contacts of the senses (mātrā-sparśa), son of Kuntī, give cold and heat, pleasure and pain; they come and go and are impermanent (anitya). Endure them (titikṣasva), Bhārata.

> mātrāsparśāstu kaunteya śītoṣṇasukhaduḥkhadāḥ|
āgamāpāyino'nityāstāṃstitikṣasva bhārata

### 2.14-15 <a id="tea-bhagavad-gita-2-14-15"></a>
`sourced` · confidence high

The contacts of the senses with their objects, giving cold and heat, pleasure and pain, come and go and are impermanent; endure them. The person whom these do not disturb, the same in pleasure and pain, the steady one, is fit for immortality.

### 2.16 <a id="tea-bhagavad-gita-2-16"></a>
`text-verified` · confidence moderate

Kṛṣṇa: of what is not (asat — the non-existent or unreal) there is no being (bhāva); of what is (sat — the existent or real) there is no non-being (abhāva). The conclusion (anta) about both of these has been seen by those who see the truth (tattvadarśin).

> nāsato vidyate bhāvo nābhāvo vidyate sataḥ|
ubhayorapi dṛṣṭo'ntastvanayostattvadarśibhiḥ

### 2.20 <a id="tea-bhagavad-gita-2-20"></a>
`text-verified` · confidence moderate

Kṛṣṇa: it is not born, nor does it ever die; it has not come to be, nor will it come to be again (or: having been, it will not again cease to be). Unborn (aja), eternal (nitya), everlasting (śāśvata), primeval (purāṇa), it is not slain when the body is slain.

> na jāyate mriyate vā kadāci
nnāyaṃ bhūtvā bhavitā vā na bhūyaḥ|
ajo nityaḥ śāśvato'yaṃ purāṇo
na hanyate hanyamāne śarīre

### 2.22 <a id="tea-bhagavad-gita-2-22"></a>
`text-verified` · confidence high

Kṛṣṇa: as a man casts off worn-out garments and takes other, new ones, so the embodied one (dehin) casts off worn-out bodies and passes into other, new ones.

### 2.42-44 <a id="tea-bhagavad-gita-2-42-44"></a>
`sourced` · confidence high

`text-verified` · confidence moderate

### 2.54 <a id="tea-bhagavad-gita-2-54"></a>
`text-verified` · confidence high

Arjuna: what is the description (bhāṣā) of one whose wisdom is steady (sthita-prajña), who is established in samādhi (samādhi-stha), Keśava? How does one of steady thought (sthita-dhī) speak, how does he sit, how does he move?

> sthitaprajñasya kā bhāṣā samādhisthasya keśava|

_level: unmarked · standpoint: seeker · path: knowledge, meditation · stage: realized (sthitaprajña) · types: karma-liberation, consciousness-mind_

terms: [sthitaprajña](../terms/sthitaprajna.md), [sthitadhī](../terms/sthitadhi.md), [samādhi](../terms/samadhi.md) · concepts: [The person of steady wisdom (sthitaprajña)](../concepts/sthitaprajna.md) · teachers: [Kṛṣṇa (Vāsudeva)](../teachers/krsna.md), [Arjuna (Pārtha, Dhanaṃjaya)](../teachers/arjuna.md)

### 2.54-72 <a id="tea-bhagavad-gita-2-54-72"></a>
`sourced` · confidence high

The person of steady wisdom (sthitaprajña): asked how such a one speaks, sits and walks, Kṛṣṇa describes one who has given up all desires and is content in the self by the self; unperturbed in sorrow, without longing in pleasure, free of passion, fear and anger; unattached everywhere; withdrawing the senses from their objects as a tortoise its limbs; for whom even the taste for objects ceases on seeing the supreme; who, restraining the senses, sits intent on the Lord; who moves among objects without attraction and aversion and attains serenity; who is awake in what is night for all beings; who, like the ocean filled by rivers, is unmoved by desires entering him; who lives without longing, without 'mine' and without ego — this is the brāhmī sthiti, and one established in it even at death attains brahmanirvāṇa.

_level: conventional · standpoint: seeker · path: knowledge, meditation · stage: realized (sthitaprajña) · types: consciousness-mind, karma-liberation, powers-experiences_

terms: [sthitaprajña](../terms/sthitaprajna.md), [brāhmī sthiti](../terms/brahmi-sthiti.md), [brahmanirvāṇa](../terms/brahma-nirvana.md) · concepts: [The person of steady wisdom (sthitaprajña)](../concepts/sthitaprajna.md), [Liberation while living](../concepts/liberation-while-living.md)

_Superseded by [2.54](bhagavad-gita.md#tea-bhagavad-gita-2-54)_

### 2.55 <a id="tea-bhagavad-gita-2-55"></a>
`text-verified` · confidence high

Kṛṣṇa: when one gives up all the desires (kāma) that have entered the mind (manas), Pārtha, content in the self alone, by the self (ātmany evātmanā tuṣṭaḥ), then he is called one of steady wisdom.

> śrī bhagavānuvāca
prajahāti yadā kāmān sarvān pārtha manogatān|
ātmanyevātmanā tuṣṭaḥ sthitaprajñastadocyate

_level: unmarked · standpoint: experiential · path: knowledge · stage: realized (sthitaprajña) · types: karma-liberation, consciousness-mind_

terms: [sthitaprajña](../terms/sthitaprajna.md), [kāma](../terms/kama.md), [ātman](../terms/atman.md), [manas](../terms/manas.md) · concepts: [The person of steady wisdom (sthitaprajña)](../concepts/sthitaprajna.md) · obstacles: [Desire (kāma)](../obstacles/kama.md) · teachers: [Kṛṣṇa (Vāsudeva)](../teachers/krsna.md), [Arjuna (Pārtha, Dhanaṃjaya)](../teachers/arjuna.md)

### 2.56 <a id="tea-bhagavad-gita-2-56"></a>
`text-verified` · confidence high

Kṛṣṇa: one whose mind is not shaken in sorrows, who has no craving (spṛhā) in pleasures, from whom passion (rāga), fear and anger (krodha) have gone — he is called a sage (muni) of steady thought.

> duḥkheṣvanudvignamanāḥ sukheṣu vigataspṛhaḥ|
vītarāgabhayakrodhaḥ sthitadhīrmunirucyate

One whose mind is not agitated in sorrows and who is free of longing in pleasures, from whom passion, fear and anger have gone, is called a sage of steady mind; one who is unattached everywhere, neither rejoicing nor hating on meeting with good or ill, has established wisdom.

_level: conventional · standpoint: seeker · path: knowledge · stage: realized (sthitadhī muni) · types: consciousness-mind, ethics_

`text-verified` · confidence high

Kṛṣṇa: he who is without attachment (anabhisneha) everywhere, who, meeting this or that, pleasant or unpleasant (śubhāśubha), neither rejoices nor hates — his wisdom is firmly established (prajñā pratiṣṭhitā).

### 2.61 <a id="tea-bhagavad-gita-2-61"></a>

Kṛṣṇa: restraining them all, let him sit yoked (yukta āsīta), intent on me (mat-para); for he whose senses are under control — his wisdom is firmly established.

vaśe hi yasyendriyāṇi tasya prajñā pratiṣṭhitā

### 2.62 <a id="tea-bhagavad-gita-2-62"></a>
`text-verified` · confidence high

Kṛṣṇa: for a person who dwells on (dhyāyataḥ) sense-objects, attachment (saṅga) to them arises; from attachment desire (kāma) is born; from desire, anger (krodha) arises;

> dhyāyato viṣayānpuṃsaḥ saṅgasteṣūpajāyate|
saṅgāt saṃjāyate kāmaḥ kāmātkrodho'bhijāyate

_level: conventional · standpoint: causal · path: general · stage: all · types: consciousness-mind_

### 2.62-63 <a id="tea-bhagavad-gita-2-62-63"></a>
`sourced` · confidence high

For a person dwelling on sense-objects attachment to them arises; from attachment desire is born, from desire anger; from anger comes delusion, from delusion the loss of memory, from loss of memory the destruction of understanding, and from the destruction of understanding one perishes.

> dhyāyato viṣayān puṃsaḥ saṅgas teṣūpajāyate saṅgāt saṃjāyate kāmaḥ kāmāt krodho 'bhijāyate krodhād bhavati saṃmohaḥ saṃmohāt smṛtivibhramaḥ smṛtibhraṃśād buddhināśo buddhināśāt praṇaśyati

_level: conventional · standpoint: causal · path: meditation, knowledge · stage: all · types: consciousness-mind, practice_

terms: [kāma](../terms/kama.md), [krodha](../terms/krodha.md), [moha](../terms/moha.md) · obstacles: [The chain of ruin from dwelling on objects](../obstacles/chain-from-dwelling-on-objects.md)

### 2.63 <a id="tea-bhagavad-gita-2-63"></a>
`text-verified` · confidence high

Kṛṣṇa: from anger comes delusion (saṃmoha); from delusion, confusion of memory (smṛti-vibhrama); from the loss of memory, the destruction of understanding (buddhi-nāśa); from the destruction of understanding, one perishes.

> krodhādbhavati saṃmohaḥ saṃmohātsmṛtivibhramaḥ|
smṛtibhraṃśād buddhināśo buddhināśātpraṇaśyati

_level: conventional · standpoint: causal · path: general · stage: all · types: consciousness-mind_

terms: [krodha](../terms/krodha.md), [moha](../terms/moha.md), [smṛti](../terms/smrti.md), [buddhi](../terms/buddhi.md) · concepts: [From dwelling on objects to ruin (BhG 2.62–63)](../concepts/descent-from-sense-objects-to-ruin.md) · obstacles: [Anger (krodha)](../obstacles/krodha.md), [Delusion (moha)](../obstacles/moha.md) · teachers: [Kṛṣṇa (Vāsudeva)](../teachers/krsna.md), [Arjuna (Pārtha, Dhanaṃjaya)](../teachers/arjuna.md)

`text-verified` · confidence high

Kṛṣṇa: but one who moves among sense-objects with senses free from passion and aversion (rāga-dveṣa) and under the self's control, being self-governed (vidheyātman), attains serenity (prasāda).

> rāgadveṣaviyuktaistu viṣayānindriyaiścaran|
ātmavaśyairvidheyātmā prasādamadhigacchati

### 2.64-65 <a id="tea-bhagavad-gita-2-64-65"></a>
`sourced` · confidence high

One who moves among objects with senses free of attraction and aversion and under the control of the self attains serenity (prasāda); in serenity all sorrows come to an end, for the understanding of the serene soon becomes steady.

_level: conventional · standpoint: seeker · path: meditation · stage: intermediate · types: practice, consciousness-mind_

`text-verified` · confidence high

Kṛṣṇa: in serenity (prasāda) there arises for him the ending of all sorrows; for the understanding of one whose mind is serene (prasanna-cetas) quickly becomes steady.

### 2.66 <a id="tea-bhagavad-gita-2-66"></a>
`text-verified` · confidence moderate

Kṛṣṇa: for one who is not yoked (ayukta) there is no understanding (buddhi), nor is there contemplation (bhāvanā) for the unyoked; for one without contemplation there is no peace (śānti), and for one without peace, where is happiness?

### 2.66-68 <a id="tea-bhagavad-gita-2-66-68"></a>
`sourced` · confidence high

For the unyoked there is no understanding and no contemplation (bhāvanā); without contemplation no peace; for one without peace, how can there be happiness? The mind that follows the roving senses carries away one's wisdom as the wind a ship on the waters; therefore one whose senses are withdrawn from their objects has established wisdom.

_level: conventional · standpoint: seeker · path: meditation · stage: all · types: consciousness-mind, practice_

Kṛṣṇa: for when the mind (manas) is led along by the roving senses, it carries off his wisdom (prajñā), as the wind carries off a ship on the water.

tadasya harati prajñāṃ vāyurnāvamivāmbhasi

### 3.6-7 <a id="tea-bhagavad-gita-3-6-7"></a>
`sourced` · confidence high

One who restrains the organs of action but sits dwelling in mind on sense-objects is deluded and called a hypocrite; but one who controls the senses by the mind and, unattached, undertakes the yoga of action with the organs of action, excels.

_level: conventional · standpoint: seeker · path: action · stage: intermediate · types: practice, ethics_

### 3.10-16 <a id="tea-bhagavad-gita-3-10-16"></a>
`sourced` · confidence high

In the beginning Prajāpati created beings together with sacrifice and said: by this you shall multiply; nourish the gods with it and they will nourish you; one who enjoys their gifts without offering to them is a thief; the good who eat the remains of sacrifice are freed from all faults, while those who cook only for themselves eat sin; beings come from food, food from rain, rain from sacrifice, sacrifice from action, action from Brahman (the Veda), and Brahman from the Imperishable — so the all-pervading Brahman is established in sacrifice; one who does not turn the wheel thus set in motion lives in vain.

_level: conventional · standpoint: ritual · path: ritual, action · stage: all · types: world-fate, practice, ethics_

### 3.25-26 <a id="tea-bhagavad-gita-3-25-26"></a>
`sourced` · confidence high

As the unwise act attached to action, so the wise should act unattached, wishing to hold the world together; the wise should not unsettle the understanding of the ignorant who are attached to action, but, acting yoked, should engage them in all actions.

### 3.36-39 <a id="tea-bhagavad-gita-3-36-39"></a>
`sourced` · confidence high

Arjuna asks what impels a person to sin even against his will; Kṛṣṇa answers: it is desire, it is anger, born of rajas, all-devouring and very sinful — know it as the enemy here; as fire is covered by smoke, a mirror by dust and an embryo by the womb, so knowledge is covered by it — the constant enemy of the wise in the form of desire, an insatiable fire.

_level: conventional · standpoint: causal · path: general · stage: all · types: consciousness-mind_

`text-verified` · confidence high

Kṛṣṇa: the senses, they say, are high (para); higher than the senses is the mind (manas); higher than the mind is the understanding (buddhi); but what is higher than the understanding is he (saḥ).

> indriyāṇi parāṇyāhurindriyebhyaḥ paraṃ manaḥ|
manasastu parā buddhiryo buddheḥ paratastu saḥ

### 4.19-23 <a id="tea-bhagavad-gita-4-19-23"></a>
`sourced` · confidence high

One whose undertakings are free of desire and intention, whose actions are burnt in the fire of knowledge, the wise call learned; giving up attachment to fruits, ever content and dependent on nothing, he does nothing though engaged in action; without expectation, self-controlled, having given up all possessions, doing merely bodily action, he incurs no fault; content with what comes unsought, beyond the pairs of opposites, free of envy, the same in success and failure, he is not bound even when acting; for one free of attachment, liberated, whose mind is established in knowledge, acting for sacrifice, action dissolves entirely.

_level: bridging · standpoint: seeker · path: action, knowledge · stage: advanced · types: karma-liberation, practice_

concepts: [Action without attachment to its fruit](../concepts/niskama-karma.md), [Liberation while living](../concepts/liberation-while-living.md) · obstacles: [Attachment to the fruits of action; making the fruit one's motive (karmaphala-hetu)](../obstacles/attachment-to-fruits.md)

_Superseded by [4.19](bhagavad-gita.md#tea-bhagavad-gita-4-19)_

### 4.25-30 <a id="tea-bhagavad-gita-4-25-30"></a>
`sourced` · confidence high

The kinds of sacrifice: some yogins offer sacrifice to the gods; others offer sacrifice by sacrifice into the fire of Brahman; some offer hearing and the other senses into the fires of restraint, others sound and the other objects into the fires of the senses; others offer all the actions of the senses and of the breath into the fire of the yoga of self-restraint kindled by knowledge; others sacrifice with material things, with austerity, with yoga, and ascetics of strict vows with study and knowledge; others, devoted to breath-control, offer the in-breath into the out-breath and the out-breath into the in-breath, restraining their courses; others, regulating food, offer breaths into breaths — all these know sacrifice and their faults are destroyed by sacrifice.

_level: conventional · standpoint: ritual · path: ritual, meditation, body-breath · stage: all · types: practice_

terms: [yajña](../terms/yajna.md), [prāṇāyāma](../terms/pranayama.md), [prāṇa](../terms/prana.md), [apāna](../terms/apana.md), [tapas](../terms/tapas.md) · concepts: [Sacrifice reinterpreted (yajña)](../concepts/yajna-reinterpreted.md) · practices: [Breath as sacrifice: offering prāṇa and apāna](../practices/prana-apana-offering.md), [Restraint and withdrawal of the senses](../practices/indriya-nigraha.md)

_Superseded by [4.25](bhagavad-gita.md#tea-bhagavad-gita-4-25)_

### 4.37 <a id="tea-bhagavad-gita-4-37"></a>
`text-verified` · confidence high

Kṛṣṇa: as a kindled fire reduces firewood to ashes, Arjuna, so the fire of knowledge (jñānāgni) reduces all actions (karma) to ashes.

> yathaidhāṃsi samiddho'gnirbhasmasātkurute'rjuna|
jñānāgniḥ sarvakarmāṇi bhasmasātkurute tathā

_level: conventional · standpoint: causal · path: knowledge · stage: all · types: karma-liberation_

### 4.38 <a id="tea-bhagavad-gita-4-38"></a>
`text-verified` · confidence high

Kṛṣṇa: indeed, nothing here is as purifying (pavitra) as knowledge. One perfected in yoga (yoga-saṃsiddha) finds it by himself within himself (ātmani) in due time.

> na hi jñānena sadṛśaṃ pavitramiha vidyate|
tatsvayaṃ yogasaṃsiddhaḥ kālenātmani vindati

### 4.39 <a id="tea-bhagavad-gita-4-39"></a>
`text-verified` · confidence high

Kṛṣṇa: one who has faith (śraddhāvān), who is intent on it (tatpara) and has restrained the senses (saṃyatendriya) obtains knowledge; having obtained knowledge, he soon attains supreme peace (parā śānti).

> śraddhāvā~llabhate jñānaṃ tatparaḥ saṃyatendriyaḥ|
jñānaṃ labdhvā parāṃ śāntimacireṇādhigacchati

### 4.40 <a id="tea-bhagavad-gita-4-40"></a>
`text-verified` · confidence high

Kṛṣṇa: the ignorant (ajña), the faithless (aśraddadhāna) and the one of doubting mind (saṃśayātman) perish; for the doubter there is neither this world nor the next, nor happiness.

> ajñaścāśraddadhānaśca saṃśayātmā vinaśyati|
nāyaṃ loko'sti na paro na sukhaṃ saṃśayātmanaḥ

### 4.41 <a id="tea-bhagavad-gita-4-41"></a>
`text-verified` · confidence high

Kṛṣṇa: actions do not bind the self-possessed one (ātmavat), Dhanañjaya, who has renounced actions through yoga (yoga-saṃnyasta-karman) and whose doubt is cut asunder by knowledge.

### 4.41-42 <a id="tea-bhagavad-gita-4-41-42"></a>
`sourced` · confidence high

Actions do not bind one who has renounced action through yoga and whose doubt is cut by knowledge; therefore, cutting with the sword of knowledge this doubt born of ignorance that lies in your heart, resort to yoga and arise.

`text-verified` · confidence high

Kṛṣṇa: therefore, having cut with the sword of knowledge this doubt of yours (ātmanaḥ), born of ignorance (ajñāna) and lodged in the heart, resort to yoga — stand up, Bhārata!

`text-verified` · confidence high

Arjuna: you praise the renunciation (saṃnyāsa) of actions, Kṛṣṇa, and then again yoga. Tell me definitely which one of these two is better (śreyas).

> saṃnyāsaṃ karmaṇāṃ kṛṣṇa punaryogaṃ ca śaṃsasi|
yacchreya etayorekaṃ tanme brūhi suniśicatam

### 5.1-2 <a id="tea-bhagavad-gita-5-1-2"></a>
`sourced` · confidence high

Arjuna asks which is better, the renunciation of actions or their yoga; Kṛṣṇa answers that both lead to the highest good, but of the two the yoga of action excels the renunciation of action.

`text-verified` · confidence high

Kṛṣṇa: renunciation (saṃnyāsa) and the yoga of action (karma-yoga) both bring about the highest good (niḥśreyasa); but of the two, the yoga of action is superior to the renunciation of action (karma-saṃnyāsa).

yoginaḥ karma kurvanti saṅgaṃ tyaktvā''tmaśuddhaye

### 5.11-12 <a id="tea-bhagavad-gita-5-11-12"></a>
`sourced` · confidence high

Yogins perform action with body, mind, understanding and the senses alone, abandoning attachment, for the purification of the self; the yoked one, abandoning the fruit of action, attains lasting peace, while the unyoked, attached to the fruit through desire, is bound.

_level: conventional · standpoint: seeker · path: action · stage: all · types: practice_

practices: [Karma-yoga: performing one's duty without attachment to the fruit](../practices/karma-yoga.md) · obstacles: [Attachment to the fruits of action; making the fruit one's motive (karmaphala-hetu)](../obstacles/attachment-to-fruits.md)

### 5.12 <a id="tea-bhagavad-gita-5-12"></a>
`text-verified` · confidence high

Kṛṣṇa: the yoked one (yukta), abandoning the fruit of action, attains the peace of steadfastness (naiṣṭhikī śānti); the unyoked (ayukta), attached to the fruit through the drive of desire (kāmakāra), is bound.

> yuktaḥ karmaphalaṃ tyaktvā śāntimāpnoti naiṣṭhikīm|
ayuktaḥ kāmakāreṇa phale sakto nibadhyate

`text-verified` · confidence high

Kṛṣṇa: having renounced all actions with the mind (manasā), the self-controlled (vaśī) embodied one (dehin) sits at ease in the city of nine gates (navadvāra pura), neither acting nor causing action.

> sarvakarmāṇi manasā saṃnyasyāste sukhaṃ vaśī|
navadvāre pure dehī naiva kurvanna kārayan

### 5.14 <a id="tea-bhagavad-gita-5-14"></a>
`text-verified` · confidence moderate

Kṛṣṇa: the master (prabhu) does not create agency (kartṛtva) for the world, nor actions, nor the connection of action with its fruit; it is inherent nature (svabhāva) that is at work.

> na kartṛtvaṃ na karmāṇi lokasya sṛjati prabhuḥ|
na karmaphalasaṃyogaṃ svabhāvastu pravartate

### 5.14-15 <a id="tea-bhagavad-gita-5-14-15"></a>
`sourced` · confidence high

The Lord does not create agency or actions for the world, nor the union of action with its fruit; it is inherent nature (svabhāva) that acts; the all-pervading one takes on no one's sin or merit; knowledge is veiled by ignorance, and by that beings are deluded.

_level: bridging · standpoint: causal · path: knowledge · stage: all · types: karma-liberation, world-fate_

### 5.15 <a id="tea-bhagavad-gita-5-15"></a>
`text-verified` · confidence moderate

Kṛṣṇa: the pervading, mighty one (vibhu) takes on no one's evil (pāpa) nor anyone's good deed (sukṛta). Knowledge is covered by ignorance (ajñāna); by that, creatures are deluded.

> nādatte kasyacitpāpaṃ na caiva sukṛtaṃ vibhuḥ|
ajñānenāvṛtaṃ jñānaṃ tena muhyanti jantavaḥ

### 5.16 <a id="tea-bhagavad-gita-5-16"></a>
`text-verified` · confidence high

Kṛṣṇa: but for those in whom that ignorance has been destroyed by knowledge of the self (ātmanaḥ), knowledge, like the sun, illumines that highest (tat param).

> jñānena tu tadajñānaṃ yeṣāṃ nāśitamātmanaḥ|
teṣāmādityavajjñānaṃ prakāśayati tatparam

### 5.16-17 <a id="tea-bhagavad-gita-5-16-17"></a>
`sourced` · confidence high

But for those whose ignorance is destroyed by knowledge of the self, that knowledge, like the sun, reveals the supreme; with their understanding in That, their self That, established in That, devoted to That, they go to non-return, their faults shaken off by knowledge.

_level: ultimate · standpoint: seeker · path: knowledge · stage: advanced · types: karma-liberation_

`text-verified` · confidence high

Kṛṣṇa: those whose intellect (buddhi) is on That (tad-buddhi), whose self is set on That (tad-ātman), who are established in That (tan-niṣṭha) and have That as their final goal (tat-parāyaṇa), their stains (kalmaṣa) shaken off by knowledge, go to non-return (apunarāvṛtti).

> tadbuddhayastadātmānastanniṣṭhāstatparāyaṇāḥ|
gacchantyapunarāvṛttiṃ jñānanirdhūtakalmaṣāḥ

### 5.18 <a id="tea-bhagavad-gita-5-18"></a>
`text-verified` · confidence high

Kṛṣṇa: even here, the created world (sarga) is overcome by those whose mind is established in sameness (sāmya); for Brahman is flawless (nirdoṣa) and the same (sama); therefore they are established in Brahman.

### 5.20 <a id="tea-bhagavad-gita-5-20"></a>
`text-verified` · confidence high

Kṛṣṇa: one should not rejoice on obtaining what is pleasant nor be agitated on obtaining what is unpleasant: steady in intellect (sthira-buddhi), undeluded, the knower of Brahman (brahmavid) is established in Brahman.

> na prahṛṣyetpriyaṃ prāpya nodvijetprāpya cāpriyam|

### 5.20-21 <a id="tea-bhagavad-gita-5-20-21"></a>
`sourced` · confidence high

One should not rejoice on obtaining the pleasant nor be agitated on obtaining the unpleasant; steady of understanding, undeluded, the knower of Brahman is established in Brahman; unattached to external contacts he finds the happiness that is in the self, and, yoked in the yoga of Brahman, enjoys imperishable happiness.

_level: ultimate · standpoint: experiential · path: knowledge, meditation · stage: realized · types: consciousness-mind_

`text-verified` · confidence high

Kṛṣṇa: one whose self is unattached to external contacts (bāhya-sparśa) finds the happiness that is in the self; with his self yoked in the yoga of Brahman (brahma-yoga), he enjoys imperishable happiness (akṣaya sukha).

> bāhyasparśeṣvasaktātmā vindatyātmani yatsukham|
sa brahmayogayuktātmā sukhamakṣayamaśnute

### 5.22 <a id="tea-bhagavad-gita-5-22"></a>
`text-verified` · confidence high

Kṛṣṇa: for the enjoyments (bhoga) born of contact (saṃsparśa) are wombs of sorrow (duḥkha); they have a beginning and an end, son of Kuntī; the wise one does not delight in them.

> ye hi saṃsparśajā bhogā duḥkhayonaya eva te|
ādyantavantaḥ kaunteya na teṣu ramate budhaḥ

### 5.22-23 <a id="tea-bhagavad-gita-5-22-23"></a>
`sourced` · confidence high

The enjoyments born of contact are wombs of sorrow, having a beginning and an end; the wise do not delight in them. One who can withstand here, before release from the body, the impulse born of desire and anger is yoked and happy.

`text-verified` · confidence high

Kṛṣṇa: one who is able, here, before release from the body, to withstand the surge (vega) that arises from desire (kāma) and anger (krodha) — he is yoked (yukta), he is a happy person.

> śaknotīhaiva yaḥ soḍhuṃ prākśarīravimokṣaṇāt|
kāmakrodhodbhavaṃ vegaṃ sa yuktaḥ sa sukhī naraḥ

`text-verified` · confidence high

Kṛṣṇa: one who has happiness within (antaḥsukha), delight within (antarārāma) and likewise light within (antarjyotis) — that yogin, having become Brahman (brahmabhūta), attains the nirvāṇa of Brahman (brahma-nirvāṇa).

### 5.24-26 <a id="tea-bhagavad-gita-5-24-26"></a>

One whose happiness, delight and light are within becomes Brahman and attains brahmanirvāṇa; the seers whose faults are destroyed, whose doubts are cut, who are self-controlled and delight in the welfare of all beings attain brahmanirvāṇa; for ascetics freed from desire and anger, with minds controlled and knowing the self, brahmanirvāṇa is near on every side.

Kṛṣṇa: seers (ṛṣi) whose stains (kalmaṣa) are destroyed, whose doubts are cut (chinna-dvaidha: dvaidha, 'twofoldness', also taken as dualities), who are self-restrained and delight in the welfare of all beings (sarvabhūtahite ratāḥ), attain brahma-nirvāṇa.

### 5.27-28 <a id="tea-bhagavad-gita-5-27-28"></a>
`text-verified` · confidence high

Kṛṣṇa: shutting out external contacts, fixing the gaze between the brows, evening out prāṇa and apāna as they move within the nostrils, the sage who has restrained senses, mind and intellect, is intent on liberation and is free of desire, fear and anger, is forever liberated.

### 5.29 <a id="tea-bhagavad-gita-5-29"></a>
`text-verified` · confidence high

Kṛṣṇa: knowing me as the enjoyer (bhoktṛ) of sacrifices and austerities, the great lord of all the worlds (sarva-loka-maheśvara), the friend (suhṛd) of all beings, one attains peace (śānti).

> bhoktāraṃ yajñatapasāṃ sarvalokamaheśvaram|
suhṛdaṃ sarvabhūtānāṃ jñātvā māṃ śāntimṛcchati

### 6.1 <a id="tea-bhagavad-gita-6-1"></a>
`text-verified` · confidence high

Kṛṣṇa: one who does the action that is to be done (kārya karma) without depending on the fruit of action is a renunciant (saṃnyāsin) and a yogin — not the one without the sacred fire (niragni), nor the one without rites (akriya).

> śrī bhagavānuvāca
anāśritaḥ karmaphalaṃ kāryaṃ karma karoti yaḥ|
sa saṃnyāsī ca yogī ca na niragnirna cākriyaḥ

_level: conventional · standpoint: seeker · path: action · stage: all · types: practice, dispute, ethics_

terms: [saṃnyāsa](../terms/samnyasa.md), [yoga](../terms/yoga.md), [phala](../terms/phala.md), [saṃnyāsin](../terms/samnyasin.md), [karmaphala-tyāga](../terms/karmaphala-tyaga.md) · concepts: [Renunciation (saṃnyāsa) in the principal Upaniṣads](../concepts/sannyasa.md) · practices: [Karma-yoga: performing one's duty without attachment to the fruit](../practices/karma-yoga.md), [Relinquishing the fruit of all actions](../practices/karmaphala-tyaga.md) · obstacles: [Attachment to the fruits of action; making the fruit one's motive (karmaphala-hetu)](../obstacles/attachment-to-fruits.md) · teachers: [Kṛṣṇa (Vāsudeva)](../teachers/krsna.md) · disputes: [Is the renunciation of action or the yoga of action better? ### 6.1-2 <a id="tea-bhagavad-gita-6-1-2"></a>
`sourced` · confidence high

One who performs the action that is to be done without depending on its fruit is a renouncer and a yogin — not one who is without the sacred fire and without rites; what they call renunciation know to be yoga, for no one becomes a yogin without renouncing intention (saṃkalpa).

_level: bridging · standpoint: seeker · path: action, meditation · stage: all · types: practice_

### 6.2 <a id="tea-bhagavad-gita-6-2"></a>
`text-verified` · confidence high

Kṛṣṇa: what they call renunciation (saṃnyāsa), know that to be yoga, Pāṇḍava; for no one becomes a yogin without having renounced intention (saṅkalpa).

> yaṃ saṃnyāsamiti prāhuryogaṃ taṃ viddhi pāṇḍava|
na hyasaṃnyastasaṅkalpo yogī bhavati kaścana

_level: conventional · standpoint: seeker · path: action, meditation · stage: all · types: practice_

### 6.3 <a id="tea-bhagavad-gita-6-3"></a>
`text-verified` · confidence high

Kṛṣṇa: for the sage (muni) who wishes to ascend to yoga (ārurukṣu), action (karma) is said to be the means (kāraṇa); for that same one, once he has ascended to yoga (yogārūḍha), calm (śama) is said to be the means.

> ārurukṣormuneryogaṃ karma kāraṇamucyate|
yogārūḍhasya tasyaiva śamaḥ kāraṇamucyate

_level: conventional · standpoint: seeker · path: action, meditation · stage: all (ārurukṣu (one wishing to ascend to yoga) → yogārūḍha (one who has ascended to yoga)) · types: practice_

terms: [ārurukṣu](../terms/aruruksu.md), [yogārūḍha](../terms/yogarudha.md), [śama](../terms/sama.md), [karma](../terms/karma.md), [muni](../terms/muni.md) · practices: [Karma-yoga: performing one's duty without attachment to the fruit](../practices/karma-yoga.md) · teachers: [Kṛṣṇa (Vāsudeva)](../teachers/krsna.md)

### 6.3-4 <a id="tea-bhagavad-gita-6-3-4"></a>
`sourced` · confidence high

For the sage who wishes to ascend to yoga, action is said to be the means; for the same, once he has ascended, quiescence (śama) is said to be the means; when one is attached neither to sense-objects nor to actions and has renounced all intentions, one is said to have ascended to yoga.

_level: conventional · standpoint: seeker · path: action, meditation · stage: all (ārurukṣu / yogārūḍha) · types: practice_

terms: [ārurukṣu](../terms/aruruksu.md), [yogārūḍha](../terms/yogarudha.md)

_Superseded by [6.3](bhagavad-gita.md#tea-bhagavad-gita-6-3)_

### 6.4 <a id="tea-bhagavad-gita-6-4"></a>
`text-verified` · confidence high

Kṛṣṇa: when one is attached neither to the objects of the senses nor to actions, and has renounced all intentions (sarva-saṅkalpa-saṃnyāsin), then he is said to have ascended to yoga (yogārūḍha).

> yadā hi nendriyārtheṣu na karmasvanuṣajjate|
sarvasaṅkalpasaṃnyāsī yogārūḍhastadocyate

_level: conventional · standpoint: seeker · path: meditation, action · stage: advanced (yogārūḍha) · types: practice, karma-liberation, consciousness-mind_

### 6.5 <a id="tea-bhagavad-gita-6-5"></a>
`text-verified` · confidence high

Kṛṣṇa: one should lift up the self by the self (ātmanā ātmānam) and not let the self sink; for the self alone is the friend (bandhu) of the self, and the self alone is the enemy (ripu) of the self.

> uddharedātmanā''tmānaṃ nātmānamavasādayet|
ātmaiva hyātmano bandhurātmaiva ripurātmanaḥ

_level: conventional · standpoint: seeker · path: general · stage: all · types: practice, ethics, consciousness-mind_

terms: [ātman](../terms/atman.md) · concepts: [The self as its own friend and enemy (BhG 6.5–6)](../concepts/self-as-friend-and-enemy.md) · teachers: [Kṛṣṇa (Vāsudeva)](../teachers/krsna.md)

### 6.5-6 <a id="tea-bhagavad-gita-6-5-6"></a>
`sourced` · confidence high

One should lift up the self by the self and not let the self sink; the self alone is the friend of the self and the self alone its enemy; the self is a friend to one who has conquered the self by the self, but to one who has not, the self acts with enmity like an enemy.

> uddhared ātmanātmānaṃ nātmānam avasādayet ātmaiva hy ātmano bandhur ātmaiva ripur ātmanaḥ bandhur ātmātmanas tasya yenātmaivātmanā jitaḥ anātmanas tu śatrutve vartetātmaiva śatruvat

_level: conventional · standpoint: seeker · path: general · stage: all · types: practice, consciousness-mind_

terms: [ātman](../terms/atman.md)

_Superseded by [6.5](bhagavad-gita.md#tea-bhagavad-gita-6-5)_

### 6.6 <a id="tea-bhagavad-gita-6-6"></a>
`text-verified` · confidence high

Kṛṣṇa: the self is the friend of the self for one by whom the self has been conquered by the self; but for one without (mastery of) the self (anātman), the self itself would act in enmity, like an enemy.

> bandhurātmā''tmanastasya yenātmaivātmanā jitaḥ|
anātmanastu śatrutve vartetātmaiva śatruvat

_level: conventional · standpoint: seeker · path: general · stage: all · types: practice, ethics, consciousness-mind_

terms: [ātman](../terms/atman.md) · concepts: [The self as its own friend and enemy (BhG 6.5–6)](../concepts/self-as-friend-and-enemy.md) · teachers: [Kṛṣṇa (Vāsudeva)](../teachers/krsna.md)

### 6.7 <a id="tea-bhagavad-gita-6-7"></a>
`text-verified` · confidence moderate

Kṛṣṇa: for one who has conquered himself (jitātman) and is at peace (praśānta), amid cold and heat, pleasure and pain, honour and dishonour, 'paramātmā samāhitaḥ' — either the supreme self (paramātman) is established (samāhita), or, taking 'param' adverbially, his self is supremely composed.

> jitātmanaḥ praśāntasya paramātmā samāhitaḥ|
śītoṣṇasukhaduḥkheṣu tathā mānāpamānayoḥ

_level: unmarked · standpoint: seeker · path: meditation · stage: realized · types: consciousness-mind, ultimate, ethics_

terms: [paramātman](../terms/paramatman.md), [dvandva](../terms/dvandva.md) · concepts: [Evenness of mind (samatva)](../concepts/equanimity.md) · obstacles: [The pairs of opposites (dvandva)](../obstacles/dvandva.md) · teachers: [Kṛṣṇa (Vāsudeva)](../teachers/krsna.md)

### 6.7-9 <a id="tea-bhagavad-gita-6-7-9"></a>
`sourced` · confidence high

The supreme self of one who has conquered himself and is at peace is concentrated in cold and heat, pleasure and pain, honour and dishonour; the yogin content with knowledge and realization, unshaken, senses conquered, to whom a clod, a stone and gold are the same, is called yoked; one who regards well-wishers, friends, enemies, the neutral, mediators, the hateful, kinsmen, the good and the wicked with an equal mind excels.

_level: conventional · standpoint: seeker · path: meditation · stage: advanced · types: consciousness-mind, ethics_

terms: [samatva](../terms/samatva.md), [paramātman](../terms/paramatman.md) · concepts: [Evenness of mind (samatva)](../concepts/equanimity.md)

_Superseded by [6.7](bhagavad-gita.md#tea-bhagavad-gita-6-7)_

### 6.8 <a id="tea-bhagavad-gita-6-8"></a>
`text-verified` · confidence high

Kṛṣṇa: the yogin whose self is satisfied with knowledge (jñāna) and realized knowledge (vijñāna), who stands unmoved (kūṭastha), whose senses are conquered, to whom a clod, a stone and gold are the same, is called 'yoked' (yukta).

> jñānavijñānatṛptātmā kūṭastho vijitendriyaḥ|
yukta ityucyate yogī samaloṣṭāśmakāñcanaḥ

_level: unmarked · standpoint: seeker · path: meditation, knowledge · stage: realized (yukta) · types: karma-liberation, ethics, consciousness-mind_

### 6.9 <a id="tea-bhagavad-gita-6-9"></a>
`text-verified` · confidence high

Kṛṣṇa: one whose intellect is the same (sama-buddhi) toward well-wishers, friends, enemies, the indifferent, the neutral, the hateful and kinsmen, toward the good and even toward the wicked, excels.

> suhṛnmitrāryudāsīnamadhyasthadveṣyabandhuṣu|
sādhuṣvapi ca pāpeṣu samabuddhirviśiṣyate

### 6.10 <a id="tea-bhagavad-gita-6-10"></a>
`text-verified` · confidence high

Kṛṣṇa: the yogin should constantly yoke himself (yuñjīta), staying in a secluded place (rahasi), alone, restrained in thought (citta) and in himself (yata-citta-ātman), without expectations (nirāśīḥ) and without possessions (aparigraha).

> yogī yuñjīta satatamātmānaṃ rahasi sthitaḥ|
ekākī yatacittātmā nirāśīraparigrahaḥ

_level: conventional · standpoint: seeker · path: meditation · stage: unmarked · types: practice_

terms: [nirāśīḥ](../terms/nirasih.md), [aparigraha](../terms/aparigraha.md), [yoga](../terms/yoga.md), [citta](../terms/citta.md) · practices: [The Gītā's meditation (dhyāna-yoga, BhG 6.10–32)](../practices/dhyana-yoga-gita.md), [Non-possessiveness (aparigraha)](../practices/aparigraha.md) · teachers: [Kṛṣṇa (Vāsudeva)](../teachers/krsna.md)

### 6.10-32 <a id="tea-bhagavad-gita-6-10-32"></a>
`sourced` · confidence high

The method of meditation: the yogin should constantly concentrate himself, alone in a solitary place, with mind and body controlled, without expectation or possessions; set up a firm seat in a clean place, neither too high nor too low, covered with cloth, deerskin and kuśa grass; sitting there with mind one-pointed, controlling thought and senses, practise yoga for self-purification; holding body, head and neck erect and still, gazing at the tip of the nose without looking around, serene, fearless, firm in the vow of celibacy, the mind restrained and fixed on me, sit yoked and intent on me; moderate in food, sleep, recreation and effort; when the controlled mind rests in the self alone, free from longing, it is steady like a lamp in a windless place; the mind stilled by practice comes to rest, and seeing the self by the self one is content in the self, knowing the endless happiness grasped by the understanding beyond the senses; this is yoga, the unyoking from union with sorrow; withdrawing the mind gradually by a steady understanding and fixing it on the self, one should think of nothing; wherever the restless mind wanders, bring it back under control in the self; the yogin with a calm mind attains the supreme happiness of contact with Brahman, sees the self in all beings and all beings in the self, sees me everywhere, and regards the pleasure and pain of all as his own.

_level: conventional · standpoint: seeker · path: meditation · stage: intermediate · types: practice, consciousness-mind, body-layers_

terms: [dhyāna-yoga](../terms/dhyana-yoga.md), [yoga](../terms/yoga.md) · practices: [The Gītā's meditation (dhyāna-yoga, BhG 6.10–32)](../practices/dhyana-yoga-gita.md)

_Superseded by [6.10](bhagavad-gita.md#tea-bhagavad-gita-6-10)_

### 6.20-23 <a id="tea-bhagavad-gita-6-20-23"></a>
`text-verified` · confidence high

Kṛṣṇa: yoga is to be known as that state in which the mind (citta), restrained (niruddha) by the practice of yoga, comes to rest; in which one sees the self by the self and is content in the self; in which one knows the boundless happiness grasped by the intellect (buddhi) and beyond the senses, and never wavers from the truth; having gained which one counts no other gain higher; and in which one is not shaken even by heavy sorrow. It is the disconnection from union with sorrow (duḥkha-saṃyoga-viyoga), and it is to be practised with resolve and an undejected mind (anirviṇṇa-cetas).

> yatroparamate cittaṃ niruddhaṃ yogasevayā|
yatra caivātmanā''tmānaṃ paśyannātmani tuṣyati
sukhamātyantikaṃ yattadbuddhigrāhyamatīndriyam|
vetti yatra na caivāyaṃ sthitaścalati tattvataḥ
yaṃ labdhvā cāparaṃ lābhaṃ manyate nādhikaṃ tataḥ|
yasminsthito na duḥkhena guruṇāpi vicālyate
taṃ vidyād duḥkhasaṃyogaviyogaṃ yogasaṃjñitam|
sa niścayena yoktavyo yogo'nirviṇṇacetasā

_level: unmarked · standpoint: experiential · path: meditation · stage: advanced · types: consciousness-mind, powers-experiences, practice_

terms: [yoga](../terms/yoga.md), [citta](../terms/citta.md), [niruddha](../terms/niruddha.md), [sukha](../terms/sukha.md), [duḥkha](../terms/duhkha.md), [ātman](../terms/atman.md), [buddhi](../terms/buddhi.md), [indriya](../terms/indriya.md), [tattva](../terms/tattva.md) · concepts: [The paths of the Gītā: action, knowledge, meditation and devotion](../concepts/four-yogas-of-the-gita.md) · practices: [The Gītā's meditation (dhyāna-yoga, BhG 6.10–32)](../practices/dhyana-yoga-gita.md) · teachers: [Kṛṣṇa (Vāsudeva)](../teachers/krsna.md)

### 6.37-39 <a id="tea-bhagavad-gita-6-37-39"></a>
`sourced` · confidence high

Arjuna asks what becomes of one who has faith but lacks self-control and whose mind has fallen away from yoga without reaching perfection: does he perish like a scattered cloud, fallen from both, without support?

_level: conventional · standpoint: seeker · path: meditation · stage: intermediate · types: karma-liberation_

terms: [yogabhraṣṭa](../terms/yogabhrasta.md)

Arjuna: fallen from both (ubhaya-vibhraṣṭa), does he not perish like a torn cloud, without support (apratiṣṭha), mighty-armed, bewildered on the path of Brahman?

### 6.40 <a id="tea-bhagavad-gita-6-40"></a>
`text-verified` · confidence high

Kṛṣṇa: Pārtha, neither here nor hereafter is there destruction for him; for no one who does good (kalyāṇa-kṛt), dear one (tāta), goes to a bad end (durgati).

pārtha naiveha nāmutra vināśastasya vidyate|
nahi kalyāṇakṛtkaśicaddurgatiṃ tāta gacchati

### 6.40-45 <a id="tea-bhagavad-gita-6-40-45"></a>
`sourced` · confidence high

Neither here nor hereafter is there destruction for him, for no one who does good comes to a bad end; the one fallen from yoga reaches the worlds of the meritorious, dwells there for countless years, and is born in a house of the pure and prosperous, or in a family of wise yogins — a birth rarer still; there he regains the understanding of his former body and strives again; by that former practice he is carried on even without his will; even one who merely wishes to know yoga goes beyond the Vedic word; striving diligently, cleansed of faults and perfected through many births, he reaches the highest goal.

_level: conventional · standpoint: causal · path: meditation · stage: intermediate · types: karma-liberation, death-dying_

terms: [yogabhraṣṭa](../terms/yogabhrasta.md) · concepts: [The fate of the one fallen from yoga](../concepts/fate-of-the-fallen-yogin.md)

Kṛṣṇa: having reached the worlds of those who have done merit (puṇya-kṛt) and dwelt there for countless years, the one fallen from yoga (yoga-bhraṣṭa) is born in the house of the pure and prosperous (śuci, śrīmat).

> prāpya puṇyakṛtāṃ lokānuṣitvā śāśvatīḥ samāḥ|
śucīnāṃ śrīmatāṃ gehe yogabhraṣṭo'bhijāyate

`sourced` · confidence high

### 7.12-13 <a id="tea-bhagavad-gita-7-12-13"></a>

Whatever states are of sattva, rajas or tamas, know that they come from me alone; yet I am not in them, they are in me; deluded by these three states made of the guṇas, this whole world does not know me, who am beyond them and imperishable.

### 7.16-18 <a id="tea-bhagavad-gita-7-16-18"></a>
`sourced` · confidence high

Four kinds of virtuous people worship me: the afflicted (ārta), the seeker of knowledge (jijñāsu), the seeker of wealth (arthārthin) and the one who knows (jñānin); of them the jñānin, ever yoked and devoted to the One, excels; I am exceedingly dear to him and he to me; all these are noble, but the jñānin I regard as my very self, for he, yoked, abides in me alone as the highest goal.

### 7.20-23 <a id="tea-bhagavad-gita-7-20-23"></a>
`sourced` · confidence high

Those whose knowledge is carried away by various desires resort to other deities, following various observances, constrained by their own nature; whatever form any devotee wishes to worship with faith, I make that faith of his unwavering; endowed with that faith he worships it and obtains from it his desires, which are in fact granted by me alone; but the fruit of those of little understanding is finite: worshippers of the gods go to the gods, my devotees come to me.

_level: conventional · standpoint: divine · path: devotion, ritual · stage: beginner · types: karma-liberation, practice_

### 7.29 <a id="tea-bhagavad-gita-7-29"></a>
`text-verified` · confidence high

Kṛṣṇa: those who, taking refuge in me (mām āśritya), strive for release from old age and death (jarā-maraṇa-mokṣa) know that Brahman, the adhyātma (what pertains to the self) and action (karma) — completely (kṛtsnam … akhilam).

### 7.29-30 <a id="tea-bhagavad-gita-7-29-30"></a>
`sourced` · confidence high

Those who, taking refuge in me, strive for release from old age and death know that Brahman, the whole of what concerns the self (adhyātma) and all action; those who know me together with what concerns beings, the divine and sacrifice, know me even at the time of departure, with yoked minds.

### 7.30 <a id="tea-bhagavad-gita-7-30"></a>
`text-verified` · confidence high

Kṛṣṇa: those who know me together with what pertains to beings (adhibhūta) and to the gods (adhidaiva), and together with what pertains to sacrifice (adhiyajña) — they, with thought yoked (yukta-cetasaḥ), know me even at the time of departure (prayāṇa-kāla).

> sādhibhūtādhidaivaṃ māṃ sādhiyajñaṃ ca ye viduḥ|

### 8.1 <a id="tea-bhagavad-gita-8-1"></a>
`text-verified` · confidence high

Arjuna: what is that Brahman? What is the adhyātma (what pertains to the self)? What is action (karma), Supreme Person (Puruṣottama)? And what is declared to be the adhibhūta (what pertains to beings), and what is called the adhidaiva (what pertains to the gods)?

> kiṃ tadbrahma kimadhyātmaṃ kiṃ karma puruṣottama|
adhibhūtaṃ ca kiṃ proktamadhidaivaṃ kimucyate

_level: unmarked · standpoint: seeker · path: knowledge · stage: unmarked · types: ultimate_

terms: [brahman](../terms/brahman.md), [adhyātma](../terms/adhyatma.md), [karma](../terms/karma.md), [adhibhūta](../terms/adhibhuta.md), [adhidaiva](../terms/adhidaiva.md), [Puruṣottama](../terms/purusottama.md) · concepts: [The three planes: divine, bodily, sacrificial](../concepts/adhidaiva-adhyatma-adhiyajna.md) · teachers: [Arjuna (Pārtha, Dhanaṃjaya)](../teachers/arjuna.md), [Kṛṣṇa (Vāsudeva)](../teachers/krsna.md)

### 8.1-2 <a id="tea-bhagavad-gita-8-1-2"></a>
`sourced` · confidence high

Arjuna asks what Brahman is, what adhyātma, what action, what adhibhūta and adhidaiva, who the adhiyajña in this body is and how, and how Kṛṣṇa is to be known by the self-controlled at the time of departure.

_level: conventional · standpoint: seeker · path: knowledge · stage: all · types: ultimate, death-dying_

_Superseded by [8.1](bhagavad-gita.md#tea-bhagavad-gita-8-1)_

### 8.2 <a id="tea-bhagavad-gita-8-2"></a>
`text-verified` · confidence high

Arjuna: who, and in what way, is the adhiyajña (what pertains to sacrifice) here in this body, Madhusūdana? And how are you to be known at the time of departure (prayāṇa-kāla) by those whose selves are controlled (niyatātman)?

> adhiyajñaḥ kathaṃ ko'tra dehe'sminmadhusūdana|
prayāṇakāle ca kathaṃ jñeyo'si niyatātmabhiḥ

_level: unmarked · standpoint: seeker · path: knowledge, meditation · stage: unmarked · types: ultimate, death-dying_

terms: [adhiyajña](../terms/adhiyajna.md), [antakāla](../terms/antakala.md) · concepts: [The three planes: divine, bodily, sacrificial](../concepts/adhidaiva-adhyatma-adhiyajna.md), [The last thought at death](../concepts/last-thought-at-death.md) · teachers: [Arjuna (Pārtha, Dhanaṃjaya)](../teachers/arjuna.md), [Kṛṣṇa (Vāsudeva)](../teachers/krsna.md)

### 8.3 <a id="tea-bhagavad-gita-8-3"></a>
`text-verified` · confidence moderate

Kṛṣṇa: Brahman is the supreme imperishable (akṣaraṃ paramam); own being (svabhāva) is called the adhyātma; the sending-forth (visarga) that brings about the arising of beings' states of existence (bhūta-bhāva) is termed action (karma).

> śrī bhagavānuvāca
akṣaraṃ brahma paramaṃ svabhāvo'dhyātmamucyate|
bhūtabhāvodbhavakaro visargaḥ karmasaṃjñitaḥ

_level: bridging · standpoint: analytic · path: knowledge · stage: unmarked · types: ultimate, karma-liberation_

### 8.3-4 <a id="tea-bhagavad-gita-8-3-4"></a>
`sourced` · confidence high

The Imperishable is the supreme Brahman; its own nature is called adhyātma; the creative offering that brings beings into existence is called action; adhibhūta is perishable existence; the Puruṣa is the adhidaivata; and I myself am the adhiyajña here in the body.

_level: ultimate · standpoint: analytic · path: knowledge · stage: all · types: ultimate_

terms: [akṣara](../terms/aksara.md), [brahman](../terms/brahman.md), [adhyātma](../terms/adhyatma.md), [adhibhūta](../terms/adhibhuta.md), [adhidaiva](../terms/adhidaiva.md), [adhiyajña](../terms/adhiyajna.md)

### 8.4 <a id="tea-bhagavad-gita-8-4"></a>
`text-verified` · confidence moderate

Kṛṣṇa: the adhibhūta is the perishable state (kṣaro bhāvaḥ); the adhidaivata is puruṣa ('person'); and the adhiyajña is I myself, here in the body, best of the embodied.

> adhibhūtaṃ kṣaro bhāvaḥ puruṣaścādhidaivatam|
adhiyajño'hamevātra dehe dehabhṛtāṃ vara

_level: bridging · standpoint: analytic · path: knowledge · stage: unmarked · types: ultimate_

### 8.5 <a id="tea-bhagavad-gita-8-5"></a>
`text-verified` · confidence high

Kṛṣṇa: and whoever, at the time of the end (antakāla), departs leaving the body remembering me alone, comes to my state (mad-bhāva); there is no doubt of this.

`text-verified` · confidence high

Kṛṣṇa: whatever state/being (bhāva) one remembers when at the end one gives up the body, to that very one he goes, Kaunteya, having always been shaped by that state (sadā tad-bhāva-bhāvitaḥ).

### 8.8-10 <a id="tea-bhagavad-gita-8-8-10"></a>
`sourced` · confidence high

One who meditates with a mind yoked by the yoga of practice and not straying elsewhere goes to the supreme divine Puruṣa; one who at the time of departure, with unmoving mind, yoked with devotion and the power of yoga, sets the breath fully between the eyebrows and meditates on the seer, the ancient, the ruler, subtler than the subtle, the supporter of all, of unthinkable form, sun-coloured beyond darkness, reaches that supreme divine Puruṣa.

### 8.9-10 <a id="tea-bhagavad-gita-8-9-10"></a>
`text-verified` · confidence high

Kṛṣṇa: whoever, at the time of departure (prayāṇa-kāla), with unmoving mind, yoked with devotion (bhakti) and by the power of yoga (yoga-bala), having made the breath (prāṇa) enter rightly between the brows, remembers (anusmaret) the seer (kavi), the ancient, the ruler, subtler than the subtle, the sustainer of all, of inconceivable form, sun-coloured, beyond darkness — he reaches that supreme divine Person (paraṃ puruṣaṃ divyam).

`text-verified` · confidence high

### 8.15 <a id="tea-bhagavad-gita-8-15"></a>
`text-verified` · confidence high

Kṛṣṇa: having come to me, the great souls (mahātman), who have reached the highest perfection (saṃsiddhi), do not come to rebirth (punar-janma), the impermanent abode of sorrow (duḥkha-ālaya).

> māmupetya punarjanma duḥkhālayamaśāśvatam|

### 8.15-16 <a id="tea-bhagavad-gita-8-15-16"></a>
`sourced` · confidence high

### 8.17-19 <a id="tea-bhagavad-gita-8-17-19"></a>
`sourced` · confidence high

Those who know that the day of Brahmā lasts a thousand ages and his night a thousand ages know day and night; at the coming of day all manifest things arise from the unmanifest, and at the coming of night they dissolve into that same unmanifest; this same multitude of beings, arising again and again, dissolves helplessly at the coming of night and arises at the coming of day.

### 8.23-26 <a id="tea-bhagavad-gita-8-23-26"></a>
`sourced` · confidence high

I will tell the times at which yogins depart to return or not to return: fire, light, day, the bright fortnight, the six months of the sun's northern course — departing then the knowers of Brahman go to Brahman; smoke, night, the dark fortnight, the six months of the southern course — departing then the yogin reaches the lunar light and returns; these two paths of the world, the bright and the dark, are held to be eternal: by one a person goes to non-return, by the other he returns again.

_level: conventional · standpoint: cosmic · path: general · stage: all · types: death-dying, world-fate_

### 9.2 <a id="tea-bhagavad-gita-9-2"></a>
`text-verified` · confidence high

Kṛṣṇa: it is the royal knowledge (rāja-vidyā), the royal secret (rāja-guhya), the supreme purifier; it is known by direct apprehension (pratyakṣa-avagama), in accord with dharma (dharmya), very easy to practise, and imperishable.

> rājavidyā rājaguhyaṃ pavitramidamuttamam|
pratyakṣāvagamaṃ dharmyaṃ susukhaṃ kartumavyayam

### 9.3 <a id="tea-bhagavad-gita-9-3"></a>
`text-verified` · confidence high

Kṛṣṇa: people who have no faith (aśraddadhāna) in this dharma, scorcher of foes, not reaching me, return to the path of death and saṃsāra (mṛtyu-saṃsāra-vartman).

> aśraddadhānāḥ puruṣā dharmasyāsya parantapa|

### 9.4 <a id="tea-bhagavad-gita-9-4"></a>
`text-verified` · confidence high

Kṛṣṇa: all this world is pervaded (tata) by me in my unmanifest form (avyakta-mūrti); all beings abide in me, but I am not established in them.

> mayā tatamidaṃ sarvaṃ jagadavyaktamūrtinā|
matsthāni sarvabhūtāni na cāhaṃ teṣvavasthitaḥ

### 9.4-5 <a id="tea-bhagavad-gita-9-4-5"></a>
`sourced` · confidence high

By me in my unmanifest form all this world is pervaded; all beings abide in me, but I do not abide in them; and yet beings do not abide in me — behold my divine yoga: my self sustains beings and brings them into being, yet does not abide in them.

### 9.5 <a id="tea-bhagavad-gita-9-5"></a>
`text-verified` · confidence moderate

Kṛṣṇa: and yet beings do not abide in me — behold my lordly yoga (yogam aiśvaram)! Sustaining beings (bhūta-bhṛt) and not abiding in beings, my self (ātman) brings beings into being (bhūta-bhāvana).

> na ca matsthāni bhūtāni paśya me yogamaiśvaram|

Kṛṣṇa: all beings, Kaunteya, go into my nature (prakṛti) at the close of a kalpa (kalpa-kṣaya); at the beginning of a kalpa I send them forth again.

### 9.7-10 <a id="tea-bhagavad-gita-9-7-10"></a>
`sourced` · confidence high

At the end of a kalpa all beings pass into my prakṛti, and at the beginning of a kalpa I send them forth again; resting on my own prakṛti I send forth again and again this whole multitude of beings, helpless under the power of prakṛti; these actions do not bind me, seated as one indifferent and unattached to them; with me as overseer, prakṛti brings forth the moving and the unmoving, and by this cause the world revolves.

### 9.20 <a id="tea-bhagavad-gita-9-20"></a>
`text-verified` · confidence high

Kṛṣṇa: knowers of the three Vedas (traividya), drinkers of soma (somapa), cleansed of sin, having worshipped me with sacrifices (yajñair iṣṭvā), pray for the way to heaven (svar-gati); reaching the holy (puṇya) world of the lord of the gods (surendra-loka), they enjoy in heaven the divine enjoyments of the gods.

> traividyā māṃ somapāḥ pūtapāpā
yajñairiṣṭvā svargatiṃ prārthayante|
te puṇyamāsādya surendraloka
maśnanti divyāndivi devabhogān

_level: conventional · standpoint: ritual · path: ritual · stage: unmarked · types: karma-liberation, world-fate_

terms: [trayī](../terms/trayi.md), [soma](../terms/soma.md), [yajña](../terms/yajna.md), [svarga](../terms/svarga.md), [indra](../terms/indra.md) · concepts: [Heaven (svarga)](../concepts/svarga.md) · practices: [The śrauta sacrifice (in general)](../practices/srauta-yajna.md) · obstacles: [Attachment to the rewards of ritual (vedavāda)](../obstacles/attachment-to-ritual-rewards.md) · teachers: [Kṛṣṇa (Vāsudeva)](../teachers/krsna.md), [Arjuna (Pārtha, Dhanaṃjaya)](../teachers/arjuna.md), [Indra (as student and teacher)](../teachers/indra.md) · disputes: [Are the Vedic rites that hold out heaven, enjoyment and power all there is to seek ('there is nothing else')?](../debates/bhagavad-gita-vedavada.md)

### 9.20-21 <a id="tea-bhagavad-gita-9-20-21"></a>
`sourced` · confidence high

Knowers of the three Vedas, drinkers of soma, purified of sin, worship me with sacrifices and pray for the way to heaven; reaching the holy world of Indra they enjoy the divine pleasures of the gods; having enjoyed the vast world of heaven, their merit exhausted, they enter the world of mortals; thus following the dharma of the three Vedas, desiring desires, they obtain going and returning.

_level: conventional · standpoint: ritual · path: ritual · stage: all · types: karma-liberation, dispute_

obstacles: [Attachment to the rewards of ritual (vedavāda)](../obstacles/attachment-to-ritual-rewards.md) · disputes: `dsp:works-knowledge-grace`

_Superseded by [9.20](bhagavad-gita.md#tea-bhagavad-gita-9-20)_

### 9.21 <a id="tea-bhagavad-gita-9-21"></a>
`text-verified` · confidence high

Kṛṣṇa: having enjoyed that vast heavenly world, when their merit (puṇya) is exhausted they enter the world of mortals; thus following the dharma of the three Vedas (trayī-dharma), desiring objects of desire (kāma-kāma), they obtain going and coming (gatāgata).

> te taṃ bhuktvā svargalokaṃ viśālaṃ
kṣīṇe puṇye martyalokaṃ viśanti|
eva trayīdharmamanuprapannā
gatāgataṃ kāmakāmā labhante

_level: conventional · standpoint: causal · path: ritual · stage: unmarked · types: karma-liberation, world-fate_

### 9.22 <a id="tea-bhagavad-gita-9-22"></a>
`text-verified` · confidence high

Kṛṣṇa: those people who, having no other (ananyāḥ), think of me and worship me (paryupāsate) — for them, ever devoted (nityābhiyukta), I myself carry (vahāmi) acquisition and preservation (yoga-kṣema).

> ananyāścintayanto māṃ ye janāḥ paryupāsate|
teṣāṃ nityābhiyuktānāṃ yogakṣemaṃ vahāmyaham

### 9.23 <a id="tea-bhagavad-gita-9-23"></a>
`text-verified` · confidence high

Kṛṣṇa: even those devoted to other deities (anya-devatā) who sacrifice filled with faith — they too, Kaunteya, sacrifice to me alone, but not according to the rule (avidhi-pūrvakam).

> ye'pyanyadevatā bhaktā yajante śraddhayā'nvitāḥ|
te'pi māmeva kaunteya yajantyavidhipūrvakam

_level: conventional · standpoint: divine · path: devotion, ritual · stage: unmarked · types: practice, karma-liberation_

### 9.23-25 <a id="tea-bhagavad-gita-9-23-25"></a>
`sourced` · confidence high

Even the devotees of other gods who worship them with faith worship me alone, though not according to the rule; for I am the enjoyer and lord of all sacrifices, but they do not know me in truth and therefore fall; votaries of the gods go to the gods, of the ancestors to the ancestors, of spirits to the spirits, and those who sacrifice to me come to me.

_level: bridging · standpoint: divine · path: devotion, ritual · stage: all · types: karma-liberation, practice_

`text-verified` · confidence high

Kṛṣṇa: for I am the enjoyer (bhoktṛ) and the lord (prabhu) of all sacrifices; but they do not recognize me in truth (tattvena), and therefore they fall (cyavanti).

> ahaṃ hi sarvayajñānāṃ bhoktā ca prabhureva ca|
na tu māmabhijānanti tattvenātaścyavanti te

### 9.25 <a id="tea-bhagavad-gita-9-25"></a>
`text-verified` · confidence high

Kṛṣṇa: those vowed to the gods (deva-vrata) go to the gods; those vowed to the ancestors (pitṛ-vrata) go to the ancestors; those who sacrifice to the spirits (bhūtejya; bhūta: beings, spirits) go to the spirits; and those who sacrifice to me come to me.

> yānti devavratā devān pitṛ़nyānti pitṛvratāḥ|

`text-verified` · confidence high

Kṛṣṇa: whoever offers me with devotion a leaf, a flower, a fruit, water — that, offered with devotion (bhakti-upahṛta) by one whose self is pure/controlled (prayatātman), I partake of (aśnāmi).

> patraṃ puṣpaṃ phalaṃ toyaṃ yo me bhaktyā prayacchati|

`text-verified` · confidence high

Kṛṣṇa: whatever you do, whatever you eat, whatever you offer in oblation (juhoṣi), whatever you give, whatever austerity you practise, Kaunteya — do that as an offering to me (mad-arpaṇa).

> yatkaroṣi yadaśnāsi yajjuhoṣi dadāsi yat|

`sourced` · confidence high

Whatever you do, whatever you eat, whatever you offer in sacrifice, whatever you give, whatever austerity you practise — do it as an offering to me; thus you will be freed from the bonds of action bearing good and evil fruits, and, your self yoked in the yoga of renunciation, freed, you will come to me.

Kṛṣṇa: thus you will be freed from the bonds of action (karma-bandhana) with their good and bad fruits; with your self yoked in the yoga of renunciation (saṃnyāsa-yoga), freed, you will come to me.

`text-verified` · confidence high

Kṛṣṇa: even if one of very bad conduct (su-durācāra) worships me with undivided devotion (ananya-bhāk), he is to be considered good (sādhu), for he has resolved rightly (samyag vyavasitaḥ).

`sourced` · confidence high

Even if one of very evil conduct worships me with undivided devotion, he should be considered righteous, for he has resolved rightly; he quickly becomes righteous and attains lasting peace — know for certain that my devotee does not perish.

### 10.2-3 <a id="tea-bhagavad-gita-10-2-3"></a>
`sourced` · confidence high

Neither the hosts of gods nor the great seers know my origin, for I am in every way the source of the gods and seers; one who knows me as unborn, beginningless, the great Lord of the world, is undeluded among mortals and freed from all sins.

_level: ultimate · standpoint: divine · path: knowledge · stage: all · types: ultimate_

### 10.4-5 <a id="tea-bhagavad-gita-10-4-5"></a>
`sourced` · confidence high

Understanding, knowledge, freedom from delusion, patience, truth, self-restraint, calm, pleasure and pain, being and non-being, fear and fearlessness, non-harm, equanimity, contentment, austerity, giving, fame and infamy — these various states of beings arise from me alone.

_level: ultimate · standpoint: divine · path: knowledge · stage: all · types: ultimate, consciousness-mind_

### 10.6 <a id="tea-bhagavad-gita-10-6"></a>
`sourced` · confidence moderate

The seven great seers, the four ancient ones and the Manus, whose descendants are these creatures in the world, were of my nature, born from my mind.

_level: conventional · standpoint: cosmic · path: knowledge · stage: all · types: world-fate_

### 10.8 <a id="tea-bhagavad-gita-10-8"></a>
`sourced` · confidence high

I am the origin of all; from me everything proceeds; understanding this, the wise worship me, filled with devotion.

> ahaṃ sarvasya prabhavo mattaḥ sarvaṃ pravartate iti matvā bhajante māṃ budhā bhāvasamanvitāḥ

_level: bridging · standpoint: divine · path: devotion · stage: all · types: ultimate, practice_

concepts: [Devotion (bhakti)](../concepts/bhakti.md)

### 10.9 <a id="tea-bhagavad-gita-10-9"></a>
`sourced` · confidence high

With their minds on me and their lives given to me, enlightening one another and always speaking of me, they are content and rejoice.

> maccittā madgataprāṇā bodhayantaḥ parasparam kathayantaś ca māṃ nityaṃ tuṣyanti ca ramanti ca

_level: conventional · standpoint: devotional · path: devotion, sound · stage: intermediate · types: practice_

### 10.10-11 <a id="tea-bhagavad-gita-10-10-11"></a>
`sourced` · confidence high

To those who are ever yoked and worship me with love I give the yoga of understanding by which they come to me; out of compassion for them, dwelling in their hearts, I destroy the darkness born of ignorance with the shining lamp of knowledge.

_level: bridging · standpoint: divine · path: devotion, knowledge · stage: intermediate · types: karma-liberation_

`sourced` · confidence high

`sourced` · confidence high

The catalogue of manifestations: among the Ādityas Kṛṣṇa is Viṣṇu, among lights the sun; among the Vedas the Sāmaveda, among the senses the mind, in beings consciousness (cetanā); among the Rudras Śaṃkara; among words the one syllable, among sacrifices the sacrifice of silent recitation (japa); among immovables the Himālaya, among trees the aśvattha, among the perfected the sage Kapila; among sciences the knowledge of the self, among letters the letter A; he is imperishable Time and all-seizing death; among the Vṛṣṇis Vāsudeva, among the Pāṇḍavas Arjuna, among sages Vyāsa, among poets Uśanas; of secrets he is silence, and the knowledge of knowers.

### 10.39-42 <a id="tea-bhagavad-gita-10-39-42"></a>
`sourced` · confidence high

Whatever is the seed of all beings, that I am; there is no being, moving or unmoving, that could exist without me; there is no end to my divine manifestations — what I have told is only an illustration; whatever being has glory, grace or power, know that it has sprung from a fragment of my splendour; but what need have you of this detailed knowledge? I stand, supporting this whole world with a single fragment of myself.

`sourced` · confidence high

Sañjaya describes the form: many mouths and eyes, many wondrous sights, divine ornaments and raised divine weapons, divine garlands and garments and perfumes, all-wonderful, infinite, facing every way; if the light of a thousand suns were to rise at once in the sky it might resemble the splendour of that great being; there Arjuna saw the whole universe with its many divisions gathered in one in the body of the god of gods; overcome with wonder, his hair standing on end, he bowed his head and spoke with joined palms.
